Then I noticed my clothes were getting loose, like hanging off me more than usual, and I hadn’t even realized it was happening at first.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I haven’t really been focused on gaining weight or trying to avoid it. I just wasn’t eating much, and I didn’t really notice until my clothes started fitting differently.\n\nDoctor: Have you been losing your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I just stopped really wanting or bothering with food. I wasn’t making meals or tracking anything like I usually would—mostly just coffee and protein bars for days. I didn’t even realize how little I was eating until my clothes started feeling loose.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah. After the sponsor questioned my judgment, it felt like the bottom just dropped out. I went back to my apartment and just sat there feeling hollow and ashamed. I was crying over emails I normally would’ve handled without much trouble.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I haven’t really wanted to do any of the things I usually like. I haven’t been hiking, haven’t felt like editing photos, even logging meals feels pointless. There’s a woodworking project sitting in my garage that I normally enjoy, and I haven’t even touched it. It’s weird, because those things usually help keep me grounded.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts that you’d be better off dead or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I started thinking that everyone at work would probably be better off if I just didn’t wake up. And after that meeting, I actually said out loud that I should just walk into traffic and be done with it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing or seeing things that other people don't seem to?\n\nPatient: Yeah. When I was really wired, I kept hearing the project dashboard pinging even when it wasn’t open, and I heard people calling my name from empty conference rooms. One time I saw these lines of numbers moving across my bedroom wall, like they were… I don’t know, like they were trying to tell me what to do.\n\nDoctor: When you were hearing and seeing those things, were you feeling deeply depressed at the same time?\n\nPatient: No, not at the same time. The voices and the pings and the numbers on the wall were during that really wired stretch, when I was barely sleeping and felt like I could remake the whole company. Before that, when I was more normal, I wasn’t hearing or seeing stuff. And after I crashed and got really depressed, that part wasn’t happening either.\n\nDoctor: During this depressed period, have you been moving or speaking noticeably more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed myself moving or talking slower, exactly. I mean, I felt weighed down, but I don’t know if other people would say I was noticeably slower.\n\nDoctor: During this depressed period, have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I’ve felt kind of foggy, but I can’t say if it’s real trouble concentrating or just being tired and down. I haven’t really paid close attention to that.\n\nDoctor: During this depressed period, have you lost a noticeable amount of weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I didn’t weigh myself much, but my clothes started getting loose. I’d kind of stopped paying attention to eating—skipping actual meals for days, mostly just coffee and protein bars. It was noticeable once I realized what was happening.\n\nDoctor: During this depressed period, have you been feeling worthless or excessively guilty?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It’s weird, because those things usually help keep me grounded.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts that you’d be better off dead or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I started thinking that everyone at work would probably be better off if I just didn’t wake up. And after that meeting, I actually said out loud that I should just walk into traffic and be done with it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing or seeing things that other people don't seem to?\n\nPatient: Yeah. When I was really wired, I kept hearing the project dashboard pinging even when it wasn’t open, and I heard people calling my name from empty conference rooms. One time I saw these lines of numbers moving across my bedroom wall, like they were… I don’t know, like they were trying to tell me what to do.\n\nDoctor: When you were hearing and seeing those things, were you feeling deeply depressed at the same time?\n\nPatient: No, not at the same time. The voices and the pings and the numbers on the wall were during that really wired stretch, when I was barely sleeping and felt like I could remake the whole company. Before that, when I was more normal, I wasn’t hearing or seeing stuff. And after I crashed and got really depressed, that part wasn’t happening either.\n\nDoctor: During this depressed period, have you been moving or speaking noticeably more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed myself moving or talking slower, exactly. I mean, I felt weighed down, but I don’t know if other people would say I was noticeably slower.\n\nDoctor: During this depressed period, have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I’ve felt kind of foggy, but I can’t say if it’s real trouble concentrating or just being tired and down. I haven’t really paid close attention to that.\n\nDoctor: During this depressed period, have you lost a noticeable amount of weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I didn’t weigh myself much, but my clothes started getting loose. I’d kind of stopped paying attention to eating—skipping actual meals for days, mostly just coffee and protein bars. It was noticeable once I realized what was happening.\n\nDoctor: During this depressed period, have you been feeling worthless or excessively guilty?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I felt really ashamed, like I’d messed everything up. After the sponsor questioned my judgment, it was like the bottom dropped out. I just sat in my apartment feeling hollow, and I remember crying over emails that normally I would’ve just handled.\n\nDoctor: During this depressed period, have you been sleeping much less than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I wasn’t sleeping well at all. Even when I tried to go to bed, I’d keep waking up at odd hours, and my mind would go right back to the deployment plan. By morning I felt wrecked, like I hadn’t really rested.\n\nDoctor: Did this depressed period last at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been way longer than two weeks. That hollow, ashamed feeling after the project crisis has been going on for about three months now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. After the sponsor questioned my judgment, it felt like the bottom just dropped out. I went back to my apartment and just sat there feeling hollow and ashamed. I was crying over emails I normally would’ve handled without much trouble.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I haven’t really wanted to do any of the things I usually like. I haven’t been hiking, haven’t felt like editing photos, even logging meals feels pointless. There’s a woodworking project sitting in my garage that I normally enjoy, and I haven’t even touched it. It’s weird, because those things usually help keep me grounded.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts that you’d be better off dead or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I started thinking that everyone at work would probably be better off if I just didn’t wake up. And after that meeting, I actually said out loud that I should just walk into traffic and be done with it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing or seeing things that other people don't seem to?\n\nPatient: Yeah. When I was really wired, I kept hearing the project dashboard pinging even when it wasn’t open, and I heard people calling my name from empty conference rooms. One time I saw these lines of numbers moving across my bedroom wall, like they were… I don’t know, like they were trying to tell me what to do.\n\nDoctor: When you were hearing and seeing those things, were you feeling deeply depressed at the same time?\n\nPatient: No, not at the same time. The voices and the pings and the numbers on the wall were during that really wired stretch, when I was barely sleeping and felt like I could remake the whole company. Before that, when I was more normal, I wasn’t hearing or seeing stuff. And after I crashed and got really depressed, that part wasn’t happening either.\n\nDoctor: During this depressed period, have you been moving or speaking noticeably more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed myself moving or talking slower, exactly. I mean, I felt weighed down, but I don’t know if other people would say I was noticeably slower.\n\nDoctor: During this depressed period, have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I’ve felt kind of foggy, but I can’t say if it’s real trouble concentrating or just being tired and down. I haven’t really paid close attention to that.\n\nDoctor: During this depressed period, have you lost a noticeable amount of weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I didn’t weigh myself much, but my clothes started getting loose. I’d kind of stopped paying attention to eating—skipping actual meals for days, mostly just coffee and protein bars. It was noticeable once I realized what was happening.\n\nDoctor: During this depressed period, have you been feeling worthless or excessively guilty?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I felt really ashamed, like I’d messed everything up. After the sponsor questioned my judgment, it was like the bottom dropped out. I just sat in my apartment feeling hollow, and I remember crying over emails that normally I would’ve just handled.\n\nDoctor: During this depressed period, have you been sleeping much less than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I wasn’t sleeping well at all. By morning I felt wrecked, like I hadn’t really rested.\n\nDoctor: Did this depressed period last at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been way longer than two weeks. I felt like I’d really messed things up, and it was hard to shake that feeling, but I don’t know that I was convinced in a fixed way if people told me otherwise. It was more shame and worry than being certain I’d caused some harm.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having episodes where you eat an unusually large amount of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: No, not really large amounts at once. It was more the opposite. My eating just went off the rails—I stopped tracking anything, skipped actual meals for days, and was basically getting by on coffee and protein bars. Work basically fell apart. I was missing handoffs, sending out strategy documents that people said they couldn’t follow, and it got bad enough that my manager and corporate security took me to the emergency department.
